Root cause: the issues were created correctly. The failure was caused by a transient verification read, not by malformed drafts or issue mutation.

Evidence:

- `stage-20-result.json` reports `status: complete`.
- Both ledger entries have `body_verified=true` and `linked=true`.
- Treatment issue #1 has exactly issues #3 and #4 as sub-issues.
- Control issue #2 has no sub-issues yet.
- Both current issue bodies exactly match their persisted drafts.
- GitHub reports no edits to either issue.
- The comparison also succeeds in a clean child `pwsh` process, ruling out UTF-8 decoding.
- Stage 20 verified through REST with `gh api`; `02-create-issues.ps1` immediately re-read through GraphQL-backed `gh issue view`. The most likely explanation is a short REST/GraphQL consistency window. The original response was not retained, so it cannot be proven conclusively.

There is also a smaller correctness issue: the fixture’s `TrimEnd("`n")` accepts any number of trailing newlines, while the intended contract permits only a single optional trailing newline.

Proposed fix:

1. Extract the fixture’s ledger, issue-body, linkage, and handoff verification into a reusable helper.
2. Retrieve issues consistently through:
gh api "repos/$Repository/issues/$IssueNumber"
    Use the REST sub-issues endpoint for linkage verification as well.
3. Retry read-only body verification six times at five-second intervals. Do not retry authentication, authorization, or structurally invalid JSON failures.
4. Preserve exact comparison semantics:
- normalize CRLF/CR to LF;
- permit zero or one trailing newline difference;
- do not trim spaces or arbitrary trailing newlines.
5. On persistent mismatch, write redacted diagnostics containing:
- attempt timestamps and endpoint;
- expected and actual lengths;
- SHA-256 hashes;
- first differing offset, line, and column;
- the final observed body in a redacted artifact.
6. Add a recovery parameter set to `02-create-issues.ps1`, such as:
& "$ShepherdPlugin/test/02-create-issues.ps1" `
    -CampaignMetadataDirectory "1-math-treatment-remove-before-merge" `
    -RecoverArtifactDirectory "...\prompts\shepherd-task-20-20260831-1746"
    Recovery would perform **no GitHub mutations**. It would validate the completed result and ledger, verify issues #3/#4 and their ordering, then create the missing `shepherd-test-experiment-handoff.json`.
7. Make the unattended driver explicitly resumable:
- valid handoff exists → skip stage 20;
- completed result/ledger exists without a handoff → invoke recovery;
- neither exists → run normal stage 20;
- reject ambiguous or incomplete evidence instead of creating duplicates.
8. Apply consistent REST/retry reads to the final experiment verification script. Stage 20’s skill instructions should also require bounded retries for its own immediate post-creation checks.
9. Add regression coverage for:
- stale first response followed by the correct body;
- persistent mismatch;
- CRLF differences;
- exactly one optional trailing newline;
- rejection of multiple trailing newlines;
- completed ledger recovery without issue creation;
- idempotent recovery when a valid handoff already exists.

The current experiment is safely recoverable: preserve issues #3 and #4, generate the treatment handoff from the existing `20260831-1746` artifacts, then proceed with control stage 20. Rerunning normal treatment stage 20 would risk duplicate issues and should be prevented. No source files were changed.

Incorporates resolved research, campaign lesson mode, spike artifacts, branch instructions, gating tests, persistent run artifacts, and verified sub-issue ordering. All 14 inputs are required. Skip this stage when suitable implementation issues already exist.
  shepherd-task-30-from-assignment-to-ready - Stage 30 of the shepherd-task campaign lifecycle (each issue from assignment through the boundary immediately before Ready for review). Use this skill to shepherd a child Task issue from 'assigned to Copilot' through CI approval and review-agent feedback resolution, stopping just before marking the PR as **Ready for review**.
  shepherd-task-40-from-ready-to-merged-to-base - Stage 40 of the shepherd-task campaign lifecycle (each issue from Ready for review through merge to the campaign base branch). Use this skill to shepherd a task PR from 'Ready for review' through Copilot code review, local comment resolution, and merge to the specified base branch.
  shepherd-task-50-create-post-mortem - Stage 50 of the shepherd-task campaign lifecycle (campaign post-mortem after success or failure). Create a structured post-mortem report from shepherd-task run artifacts, including metrics, timeline, failures, and actionable recommendations.
  shepherd-task-approve-workflows-and-wait-for-completion - Use this skill to approve pending workflow runs on a PR branch and wait for them to complete.

Builtin skills:
  customize-cloud-agent - Skill for customizing the Copilot cloud agent (formerly known as Copilot coding agent) environment, including copilot-setup-steps.yml configuration, preinstalling tools and dependencies, runners, and settings. Use when the user mentions copilot-setup-steps, copilot setup steps, or wants to configure the cloud agent environment.
  github-pr-media - Upload an image or video to GitHub's user attachments API and embed it in a pull request description or comment. Use when asked to add screenshots, diagrams, recordings, or other media to a PR or GitHub comment.

Fixed, committed, and pushed to `origin`.

Root cause: `gh` emitted UTF-8, but the parent PowerShell console used OEM code page 437. The first em dash was decoded as `ΓÇö`, adding two characters and making every retry compare the same corrupted text. The GitHub issue and persisted draft were actually identical.

Fix: The PowerShell verifier now temporarily forces UTF-8 for native `gh` output and restores the caller’s original encoding afterward. An OEM-console regression test covers this exact failure.

The following relevant paths are encoding-safe for this problem:

- `verify-github-issue-body.ps1` forces UTF-8 around `gh` and restores the caller’s encoding.
- The Bash verifier redirects raw REST JSON to a file before using `jq`.
- Stage-20 draft validation, result assertion, redaction, and ledger processing read files through PowerShell/.NET rather than native console decoding.
- GitHub issue/PR numbers, SHAs, states, reviewer logins, campaign IDs, and the configured experiment branches are ASCII.

Assessment: there is no code-page finding that should block the `edburns/dd-3057440-02` run with the directories and branch names you described. Read 20260831-run-treatment-control-experiment.ps1 L296:325 (30 lines read)

Thought for 15s
The user is asking, "What can be done about this?" which suggests they're looking for potential solutions rather than implementation details. I think recommending a driver-scope UTF8 setting around the command could be helpful, along with testing it with an em dash. If they're interested in a broader hardening approach, I could suggest setting UTF8 for the entire driver and any generated child scripts. Also, it seems like the current helper method could be reused, which is a plus!

The best fix is to force UTF-8 while invoking `copilot skill list`, then restore the caller’s encoding, just as `verify-github-issue-body.ps1` now does:

$previousConsoleOutputEncoding = [Console]::OutputEncoding
$previousOutputEncoding = $OutputEncoding
$utf8Encoding = [System.Text.UTF8Encoding]::new($false)

try {
    [Console]::OutputEncoding = $utf8Encoding
    $OutputEncoding = $utf8Encoding

    $skillList = @(copilot skill list 2>&1)
}
finally {
    $OutputEncoding = $previousOutputEncoding
    [Console]::OutputEncoding = $previousConsoleOutputEncoding
}

This preserves em dashes and other Unicode in descriptions while keeping the existing skill-name validation unchanged.

Other options are less desirable:

- Remove `$skillList | ForEach-Object { Write-Host $_ }`: hides the mojibake but does not fix decoding.
- Run `chcp 65001`: affects the entire console session and can disturb the caller’s environment.
- Set UTF-8 for the whole driver: provides broader protection for every native command, but requires a top-level `try/finally` to restore encoding. This is reasonable broader hardening, though larger than fixing this one display.
- Use machine-readable output: preferable in principle, but the current Copilot CLI help does not document a JSON or names-only mode for `copilot skill list`.

I recommend the narrowly scoped UTF-8 guard plus a regression test containing an em dash in mocked `copilot skill list` output. No files were changed.Thought for 4s

Todo completed 3 items
│ remove-driver-stage20-test
│ diagnose-stage25-last-exit
└ propose-stage25-fix

The driver no longer invokes `05-stage20-artifact-contract.ps1`. That change is currently uncommitted.

Root cause: `shepherd-task-25-given-list.ps1:32` executes its first native command inside a PowerShell pipeline:

$repoRoot = (& git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>$null |
    Select-Object -First 1)

Under PowerShell 7.6.5, the native command in that pipeline runs in a child scope. Its `$LASTEXITCODE` is not propagated into the script scope. Line 33 then reads an automatic variable that has never been created:

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0 -or -not $repoRoot) {

With `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest`, that produces the observed exception. I reproduced this behavior independently. Moving `git` outside the pipeline creates `$LASTEXITCODE` correctly.

The failure occurred before stage 25 created its run directory or performed any Copilot or GitHub mutation. Treatment issues #3/#4 and control issues #5/#6 were created successfully by stage 20.

Proposed fix:

1. Change stage 25 to capture native output and exit status before applying PowerShell pipeline operations:
$repoRootOutput = @(& git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>$null)
$gitExitCode = $LASTEXITCODE

if ($gitExitCode -ne 0 -or $repoRootOutput.Count -eq 0) {
    throw 'Run inside the campaign Git worktree.'
}

$repoRoot = [IO.Path]::GetFullPath(
    ([string]$repoRootOutput[0]).Trim()
)
2. Correct the same stale-exit-code pattern in `resolve-repository-remote.ps1:25-26`:
$remoteUrlOutput = @(git remote get-url $RemoteName 2>$null)
$gitExitCode = $LASTEXITCODE
$remoteUrl = $remoteUrlOutput | Select-Object -First 1
This script currently appears to work because an earlier `git remote` call usually leaves `$LASTEXITCODE=0`; the piped `git remote get-url` does not update it. It can therefore use a stale success code.
3. Harden `shepherd-task.ps1:39`, which also pipes `git rev-parse` directly into `Select-Object`. It does not currently read `$LASTEXITCODE`, but a Git failure becomes an unclear null `.Trim()` failure.
4. Add an offline stage-25 regression that launches a fresh `pwsh -NoProfile` process with StrictMode and no preexisting `$LASTEXITCODE`. Keep that test available for explicit execution; do not add it to the unattended experiment driver.
5. Extend the existing remote-resolution contract to verify that `git remote get-url` success and failure use the captured exit code rather than a stale value.

Initializing `$LASTEXITCODE=0` would hide the exception but would not fix the bug—the pipeline would continue leaving a stale status. The native command and its exit-code capture must occur outside the pipeline.!I’ll first remove only the stage-20 contract invocation from the live experiment driver. Then I’ll trace the stage-25 startup path and reproduce the unset `$LASTEXITCODE` condition without rerunning campaign mutations.

└ propose-stage25-fix

The driver no longer invokes `05-stage20-artifact-contract.ps1`. That change is currently uncommitted.

Root cause: `shepherd-task-25-given-list.ps1:32` executes its first native command inside a PowerShell pipeline:

$repoRoot = (& git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>$null |
    Select-Object -First 1)

Under PowerShell 7.6.5, the native command in that pipeline runs in a child scope. Its `$LASTEXITCODE` is not propagated into the script scope. Line 33 then reads an automatic variable that has never been created:

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0 -or -not $repoRoot) {

With `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest`, that produces the observed exception. I reproduced this behavior independently. Moving `git` outside the pipeline creates `$LASTEXITCODE` correctly.

The failure occurred before stage 25 created its run directory or performed any Copilot or GitHub mutation. Treatment issues #3/#4 and control issues #5/#6 were created successfully by stage 20.

Proposed fix:

1. Change stage 25 to capture native output and exit status before applying PowerShell pipeline operations:
$repoRootOutput = @(& git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>$null)
$gitExitCode = $LASTEXITCODE

if ($gitExitCode -ne 0 -or $repoRootOutput.Count -eq 0) {
    throw 'Run inside the campaign Git worktree.'
}

$repoRoot = [IO.Path]::GetFullPath(
    ([string]$repoRootOutput[0]).Trim()
)
2. Correct the same stale-exit-code pattern in `resolve-repository-remote.ps1:25-26`:
$remoteUrlOutput = @(git remote get-url $RemoteName 2>$null)
$gitExitCode = $LASTEXITCODE
$remoteUrl = $remoteUrlOutput | Select-Object -First 1
This script currently appears to work because an earlier `git remote` call usually leaves `$LASTEXITCODE=0`; the piped `git remote get-url` does not update it. It can therefore use a stale success code.
3. Harden `shepherd-task.ps1:39`, which also pipes `git rev-parse` directly into `Select-Object`. It does not currently read `$LASTEXITCODE`, but a Git failure becomes an unclear null `.Trim()` failure.
4. Add an offline stage-25 regression that launches a fresh `pwsh -NoProfile` process with StrictMode and no preexisting `$LASTEXITCODE`. Keep that test available for explicit execution; do not add it to the unattended experiment driver.
5. Extend the existing remote-resolution contract to verify that `git remote get-url` success and failure use the captured exit code rather than a stale value.

Initializing `$LASTEXITCODE=0` would hide the exception but would not fix the bug—the pipeline would continue leaving a stale status. The native command and its exit-code capture must occur outside the pipeline.

The PSNCPPS audit found two confirmed exit-code propagation bugs, six unsafe early-termination/output-masking pipelines, and two adjacent patterns worth correcting in the same change.

Confirmed PSNCPPS bugs

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┬───────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ File                                    │ Lines │ Problem                                   │ Consequence                                                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ scripts/shepherd-task-25-given-list.ps1 │ 32–33 │ git ... | Select-Object -First 1,         │ $LASTEXITCODE can be undefined under StrictMode—the current    │
│                                         │       │ followed by $LASTEXITCODE in the outer    │ failure.                                                       │
│                                         │       │ scope                                     │                                                                │
├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ scripts/resolve-repository-remote.ps1   │ 25–26 │ git remote get-url ... | Select-Object    │ Reads an undefined or stale status. It usually appears to work │
│                                         │       │ -First 1, followed by $LASTEXITCODE       │ because an earlier git remote command left 0 behind.           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┴───────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Unsafe output-only PSNCPPS patterns

These do not read `$LASTEXITCODE`, but the early consumer prevents reliable native completion/status handling. Native failures can be mistaken for empty output.

```
┌───────────────────────────────────┬─────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ File                              │   Lines │ Use                                                                                                     │
├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ scripts/shepherd-task.ps1         │      39 │ Determines repository root with git ... | Select-Object -First 1; failure becomes a null .Trim() error. │
├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ scripts/shepherd-task.ps1         │ 100–102 │ Finds the first linked PR from the issue timeline.                                                      │
├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ scripts/shepherd-task.ps1         │ 107–109 │ Finds the first PR whose body references the issue.                                                     │
├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ scripts/shepherd-task.ps1         │ 114–116 │ Finds the first PR matching title or branch.                                                            │
├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ scripts/shepherd-task-monitor.ps1 │ 103–105 │ Finds the first linked PR from the timeline.                                                            │
├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ scripts/shepherd-task-monitor.ps1 │ 110–112 │ Finds the first PR through body search.                                                                 │
└───────────────────────────────────┴─────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

For PR discovery, a failed `gh` request can look like “no PR found.” In `shepherd-task.ps1`, that can cause an unnecessary phase launch or a misleading “No open PR found” failure.

PSNCPPS-adjacent patterns to include in comprehensive hardening

```
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ File                                                          │ Lines │ Problem                                                                              │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ scripts/shepherd-task-monitor.ps1                             │   123 │ Pipes gh pr view directly into ConvertFrom-Json without separately capturing native  │
│                                                               │       │ status. ConvertFrom-Json drains the pipeline, so this is not the current propagation │
│                                                               │       │ bug, but CLI failure handling is ambiguous.                                          │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ skills/shepherd-task-40-from-ready-to-merged-to-base/SKILL.md │ 86–87 │ Pipes gh ... --help into Select-String -Quiet. It intentionally stops after a match  │
│                                                               │       │ and uses the Boolean result, so it is currently fail-safe, but it cannot distinguish │
│                                                               │       │ unsupported functionality from a failed gh invocation.                               │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Patterns confirmed safe

The PowerShell 7.6.5 behavior matrix showed:

- `native-command | Select-Object -First 1` → `$LASTEXITCODE` may remain undefined.
- `native-command | Out-String` → drains the stream and preserves the native status.
- `input | native-command` → the native-last command’s status propagates.
- `& child-script.ps1` where the child executes `exit N` → propagates `N`.

Therefore these existing patterns are safe from PSNCPPS:

- `verify-github-issue-body.ps1:149–150`: `gh | Out-String`, followed immediately by status capture.
- `shepherd-task.ps1:83–86`: prompt piped into `copilot`, which is last in the pipeline.
- `shepherd-task-25-given-list.ps1:88–89`: same native-last Copilot pattern.
- `get-copilot-skill-list.ps1:24–25`: array capture without a PowerShell pipeline.
- `shepherd-task-25-given-list.ps1:104–105`: `shepherd-task.ps1` explicitly ends with `exit 0` or `exit 1`, so the caller receives its exit code.
- Stage 00, stage 15, and test scripts `00`, `01`, `02`, and `04`: they run the native command first, inspect `$LASTEXITCODE`, and only then apply PowerShell transformations to the captured output.

File-only pipelines using `Get-Content`, `Get-ChildItem`, `Where-Object`, `ConvertFrom-Json`, or `Select-Object` are not PSNCPPS because no native process is involved.

Complete future fix scope

When implementing the PSNCPPS fix, I recommend changing all ten command sites across:

1. `scripts/shepherd-task-25-given-list.ps1`
2. `scripts/resolve-repository-remote.ps1`
3. `scripts/shepherd-task.ps1`
4. `scripts/shepherd-task-monitor.ps1`
5. `skills/shepherd-task-40-from-ready-to-merged-to-base/SKILL.md`

Each native command should finish into an array or string first, immediately copy `$LASTEXITCODE` into a named variable, validate that status, and only then use `Select-Object`, `ConvertFrom-Json`, or `Select-String`.

Regression coverage should include:

- A fresh StrictMode process where `$LASTEXITCODE` has never existed.
- A stale prior success followed by a failing native command.
- A stale prior failure followed by a successful native command.
- Multiple output records with `Select-Object -First 1`.
- PR-discovery API failure versus a legitimate empty result.
- Remote lookup failure.
- Reviewer-token help success, unsupported output, and command failure.

The only current source change remains the previously requested removal of the stage-20 contract invocation from the unattended driver. No PSNCPPS fixes were made.

Patterns confirmed safe

The PowerShell 7.6.5 behavior matrix showed:

- `native-command | Select-Object -First 1` → `$LASTEXITCODE` may remain undefined.
- `native-command | Out-String` → drains the stream and preserves the native status.
- `input | native-command` → the native-last command’s status propagates.
- `& child-script.ps1` where the child executes `exit N` → propagates `N`.

Therefore these existing patterns are safe from PSNCPPS:

- `verify-github-issue-body.ps1:149–150`: `gh | Out-String`, followed immediately by status capture.
- `shepherd-task.ps1:83–86`: prompt piped into `copilot`, which is last in the pipeline.
- `shepherd-task-25-given-list.ps1:88–89`: same native-last Copilot pattern.
- `get-copilot-skill-list.ps1:24–25`: array capture without a PowerShell pipeline.
- `shepherd-task-25-given-list.ps1:104–105`: `shepherd-task.ps1` explicitly ends with `exit 0` or `exit 1`, so the caller receives its exit code.
- Stage 00, stage 15, and test scripts `00`, `01`, `02`, and `04`: they run the native command first, inspect `$LASTEXITCODE`, and only then apply PowerShell transformations to the captured output.

File-only pipelines using `Get-Content`, `Get-ChildItem`, `Where-Object`, `ConvertFrom-Json`, or `Select-Object` are not PSNCPPS because no native process is involved.

Complete future fix scope

When implementing the PSNCPPS fix, I recommend changing all ten command sites across:

1. `scripts/shepherd-task-25-given-list.ps1`
2. `scripts/resolve-repository-remote.ps1`
3. `scripts/shepherd-task.ps1`
4. `scripts/shepherd-task-monitor.ps1`
5. `skills/shepherd-task-40-from-ready-to-merged-to-base/SKILL.md`

Each native command should finish into an array or string first, immediately copy `$LASTEXITCODE` into a named variable, validate that status, and only then use `Select-Object`, `ConvertFrom-Json`, or `Select-String`.

Regression coverage should include:

- A fresh StrictMode process where `$LASTEXITCODE` has never existed.
- A stale prior success followed by a failing native command.
- A stale prior failure followed by a successful native command.
- Multiple output records with `Select-Object -First 1`.
- PR-discovery API failure versus a legitimate empty result.
- Remote lookup failure.
- Reviewer-token help success, unsupported output, and command failure.

Confirmed finding

│ Classification │ Location                                                             │ Problem                             │ Impact                         │
├────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ PSNCPPS status │ skills/shepherd-task-40-from-ready-to-merged-to-base/SKILL.md:85-90  │ gh pr edit --help is piped directly │ A failed gh invocation is      │
│ masking        │                                                                      │ into Select-String -Quiet, and the  │ indistinguishable from valid   │
│                │                                                                      │ native exit status is ignored.      │ help output that lacks         │
│                │                                                                      │                                     │ @copilot.                      │
└────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘

Current code:

$supportsCopilotReviewer = gh pr edit --help |
    Select-String -SimpleMatch '@copilot' -Quiet

This did not reproduce the earlier “unset `$LASTEXITCODE`” behavior: unlike `Select-Object -First 1`, `Select-String -Quiet` preserved `$LASTEXITCODE` in testing. It is nevertheless in the PSNCPPS class because the code discards that status and collapses two distinct outcomes:

1. `gh pr edit --help` succeeded, but `@copilot` was absent.
2. `gh pr edit --help` failed.

The fix should buffer output and capture status before searching it:

$helpOutput = @(gh pr edit --help 2>&1)
$ghExitCode = $LASTEXITCODE

if ($ghExitCode -ne 0) {
    throw "SHEPHERD FAILED: could not inspect gh pr edit capabilities; gh exited $ghExitCode."
}

$supportsCopilotReviewer = [bool](
    $helpOutput | Select-String -SimpleMatch '@copilot'
)

Separate PowerShell defect discovered

`skills/shepherd-task-30-from-assignment-to-ready/SKILL.md:94-108` contains a PowerShell example using Bash’s `<<<` operator:

gh api `
  ...
  --input - <<< $body

That is not valid PowerShell, so the example cannot execute. This is not itself PSNCPPS, but its replacement needs to be PSNCPPS-safe:

$body | gh api `
    --method POST `
    -H "Accept: application/vnd.github+json" `
    -H "X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28" `
    "/repos/$REPO/issues/$TASK_ISSUE/assignees" `
    --input -

$ghExitCode = $LASTEXITCODE
if ($ghExitCode -ne 0) {
    throw "SHEPHERD FAILED: assigning issue #$TASK_ISSUE exited $ghExitCode."
}

Here the native command is the final pipeline consumer, which is a safe pattern, provided `$LASTEXITCODE` is captured immediately.

Safe exclusions

These require no production change:

- `skills/shepherd-task-20-create-issues-from-plan/SKILL.md:145-149`
- Invokes `validate-stage20-drafts.ps1`.
- The script uses terminating `throw` statements. Those propagate through `& $DRAFT_VALIDATOR`; a separate `$LASTEXITCODE` check is unnecessary.
- `skills/shepherd-task-20-create-issues-from-plan/SKILL.md:212-219`
- Captures output from `verify-github-issue-body.ps1`.
- The verifier throws on terminal failure and returns the issue object only after successful verification.
- `plugins/shepherd-task/scripts/validate-stage20-drafts.ps1`
- Contains no native commands.
- Its `Select-Object -First 1` at line 51 consumes an already materialized PowerShell array, not native-process output.
- `plugins/shepherd-task/scripts/verify-github-issue-body.ps1:149-152`
- Its native pipeline uses `Out-String`, which drains the output.
- It captures `$LASTEXITCODE` immediately and checks it before JSON parsing.

No directly invoked scripts were found beyond the PowerShell and Bash stage-20 validators and body verifiers.

PowerShell translation risks

The skills remain predominantly Bash-oriented. The most significant Windows translation risks are:

- Stage 30 PR discovery at approximately lines 128–172:
`gh ... | head -1` must not become `gh ... | Select-Object -First 1`.
- Stage 30 file counting at line 292:
native output must be captured and checked before counting.
- Stages 30 and 40 contain numerous `gh | jq` examples. A PowerShell translation must capture complete native output and its status before `ConvertFrom-Json` or other PowerShell transformations.
- The workflow-approval sub-skill has only Bash examples. Any PowerShell implementation must check each `gh run rerun` and `gh pr checks --watch` exit immediately.

These are translation hazards, not existing embedded PowerShell occurrences.

Proposed implementation

1. Fix the stage-40 capability preflight by separating native output/status capture from `Select-String`.
2. Fix the invalid stage-30 PowerShell assignment example and add immediate native-status handling.
3. Add a short PowerShell execution rule to stages 30, 40, and the workflow-approval sub-skill: buffer native output and capture `$LASTEXITCODE` before any PowerShell transformation.
4. Add `plugins/shepherd-task/test/09-skill-powershell-contract.ps1` rather than expanding `08-psncpps-contract.ps1`:
- Extract ordinary and blockquoted ````powershell` fences from shepherd-task skills.
- Strip Markdown blockquote prefixes.
- Parse every extracted block with the PowerShell AST, catching the current `<<<` defect.
- Reject native-producer pipelines into `Select-Object`, `Select-String`, or `ConvertFrom-Json`.
- Require documented native-status checks for the corrected stage-30 and stage-40 examples.
5. Keep `08-psncpps-contract.ps1` focused on executable `.ps1` files; Markdown extraction and validation are sufficiently different to warrant a separate contract.

=== TREATMENT CAMPAIGN LESSONS ===
# Campaign lessons

This file contains validated, reusable lessons for subsequent issues in this campaign.
The issue specification and repository instructions remain authoritative.

## Validated lessons

## Validated lessons from issue #3 (PR #7)

- **Applies to:** PowerShell tests run with Pester 5 through a repository-owned acceptance runner.
- **Lesson:** Put setup that defines functions or variables used by `It` blocks in a root-level `BeforeAll`, and validate through the repository runner rather than an ambient Pester installation.
- **Evidence:** CI run 33560430822 discovered tests but lost top-level setup during execution; after moving setup into `BeforeAll`, `eng/test-math-tool.ps1` imported its pinned Pester 5.7.1 and passed all eight tests at `755bf26413cc2235936d03e4164540665b36f487`.
- **Source:** both
- **Confidence:** high

## Validated lessons from issue #4 (PR #8)

- **Applies to:** PowerShell script parameters that select a finite set of CLI operations.
- **Lesson:** Use `ValidateSet` on the script-level operation parameter so unsupported operations fail before any result is written.
- **Evidence:** The `rejects unsupported operations` case in `math-tool.Tests.ps1`, run by `eng/test-math-tool.ps1`, verifies that an unsupported operation exits nonzero and writes zero stdout lines.
- **Source:** both
- **Confidence:** high
